# The Margulis–Platonov conjecture over number fields

Coordinate `EN-101-000-0000-2-2026-PR-T4-UK-EX-M1-C-AL-M-0053` | `PM-018.2` | status ACTIVE | original slug `The-Margulis-Platonov-conjecture-over-number-fields-September-23-2026` | date 2026-09-23 | derived from the openai/math preprint The-Margulis-Platonov-conjecture-over-number-fields-September-23-2026, modified (Apache-2.0). ```json
{
  "abstract_verbatim": "We prove the Margulis–Platonov conjecture over number fields. For an absolutely almost simple simply connected group, every noncentral abstract normal subgroup of its rational points is the inverse image of an open normal subgroup of the product of its anisotropic nonarchimedean local groups.",

<a id="pm-s1"></a>
## 1. Abstract and result contract

> We prove the Margulis–Platonov conjecture over number fields. For an absolutely almost simple simply connected group, every noncentral abstract normal subgroup of its rational points is the inverse image of an open normal subgroup of the product of its anisotropic nonarchimedean local groups.

- **claim-main-mp** (theorem, [source](#stmt-thm-main)): Let $k$ be a number field and $G$ an absolutely almost simple simply connected algebraic $k$-group. If $N\triangleleft G(k)$ is not contained in $Z(G(k))$, then there is an open normal subgroup $W\triangleleft H_A$ such that $$N=\delta_A^{-1}(W).$$
  - Explanation: Every noncentral abstract normal subgroup of the rational points is the preimage of an open normal subgroup of the product of the anisotropic nonarchimedean local groups.
- **claim-positive-density-csp** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-cor-positive-density-csp)): Let $k$ be a number field and $G$ an absolutely almost simple simply connected algebraic $k$-group. Let $M/k$ be the minimal Galois extension over which $G$ becomes an inner form of the split group, and let $\operatorname{Spl}(M/k)$ be the set of nonarchimedean places of $k$ that split completely in $M$. Suppose that $S$ is a set of places of $k$ which contains all archimedean places, contains no nonarchimedean place $v$ with $\mathop{\mathrm{rank}}_{k_v}G=0$, and satisfies $$d_k\bigl(S\cap\operatorname{Spl}(M/k)\bigr)>0,$$ where $d_k$ denotes Dirichlet density and the indicated density is assumed to exist. Then the $S$-congruence kernel is trivial: $$C^S(G)=\{1\}.$$ Equivalently, for any fixed faithful $k$-defined representation of $G$, the associated group $G(\mathcal O(S))$ over the ring of $S$-integers has the classical congruence subgroup property: every finite-index normal subgroup contains a principal congruence subgroup of some nonzero ideal level.
  - Explanation: Under positive Dirichlet density on completely split places, the congruence kernel is trivial and the classical congruence subgroup property holds.
- **claim-known-mp-cases** (classification, [source](#stmt-thm-known-mp)): The assertion (MP) holds except possibly for anisotropic groups of outer type $A$, trialitarian type $D_4$, and type $E_6$. In particular it holds for every inner form of $\mathop{\mathrm{SL}}_n$, over every number field.
  - Explanation: The assertion is already known outside anisotropic outer type A, trialitarian D4 and E6.
- **claim-simply-connected-hasse** (theorem, [source](#stmt-thm-foundations-hasse)): Let $H$ be a simply connected semisimple group over a number field $k$. Then $H^1(k_v,H)=1$ at every nonarchimedean place. A torsor $X$ under $H$ that has a point over every completion of $k$ has a $k$-point. Moreover, $X(k)$ is dense in $\prod_{v\in B}X(k_v)$ for every finite set $B$ of places.
  - Explanation: Local triviality of the first cohomology, a Hasse principle for torsors and weak approximation hold for simply connected semisimple groups.
- **claim-finite-defect-reduction** (theorem, [source](#stmt-prop-finite-defect)): Every abstract noncentral normal subgroup of $G(k)$ has finite index. Fix $e\geq1$, and put $$R=G(k)^e,\qquad P_0=\overline{\delta(R)}\subset G_A,\qquad V_0=\delta^{-1}(P_0),\qquad V=V_0/R.$$ Then $R$ has finite index, $P_0$ is open and normal in $G_A$, and $V$ is finite. For every positive-rank nonarchimedean place $w$, the closure of $R$ in the adelic product away from $w$ is exactly the inverse image of $P_0$.* *To prove (MP) for $G$, it suffices to show $V=1$ for every $e$. If $\gamma\in V_0$, representatives of $\gamma R$ can be chosen arbitrarily close to $1$ at any prescribed finite set of places. Away from $A$, these near-identity conditions may be replaced by arbitrary nonempty local open conditions. Finitely many nonempty Zariski open conditions may be imposed simultaneously.
  - Explanation: Noncentral normal subgroups have finite index and the defect quotient of a power subgroup is finite; it suffices to show it is trivial.
- **claim-restriction-mp** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-restriction-mp)): Let $\psi:G(k)\to F_0$ be a homomorphism to a finite group of exponent dividing $e$. Let $f:H\to G$ be a $k$-homomorphism, where $H$ is a product of restrictions of scalars of simply connected absolutely almost simple groups for which (MP) is known over their respective number fields. The homomorphism $\psi\circ f$ on $H(k)$ extends continuously to the product of the anisotropic finite local groups of those factors. Its image still has exponent dividing $e$. Consequently $\psi(f(h))=1$ if $h$ is an $e$-th power at every one of these local factors.
  - Explanation: A finite-exponent quotient restricted to a subgroup satisfying the assertion extends continuously over its anisotropic local factors and kills local powers.
- **claim-affine-sieve** (construction, [source](#stmt-lem-foundations-affine-sieve)): Let $E$ be an $N$-dimensional $k$-vector space, and let $Z\subset E$ be closed of geometric codimension at least two. Choose $d\in E(k)\setminus\{0\}$ whose point at infinity is outside the projective closure of $Z$, and let $\pi:E\to E/kd$ be the quotient map. Fix a nonarchimedean place $v_0$, linear coordinates, and a rational linear section of $\pi$. Outside a finite set of model exceptions, require that these data give an integral direct sum $E=kd\oplus E'$, with $d$ an integral basis for the first summand, that $\pi\|_Z$ remains finite on reduction, and that every geometric fiber has cardinality at most $D$.* *Let $\Sigma$ be a finite set containing $v_0$, the archimedean places, those model exceptions, and all places with residue cardinality at most $D$. For every $v\in\Sigma\setminus\{v_0\}$, prescribe a nonempty open subset $\mathcal O_v\subset E(k_v)$. There are a vector $y\in E(k)$ and elements $l\in k$ with $\|l\|_{v_0}$ arbitrarily large such that $a=y+ld$ belongs to $\mathcal O_v$ at these prescribed places, is integral outside $\Sigma$, and its reduction avoids $Z$ at every finite place outside $\Sigma$.
  - Explanation: Along a line one finds rational points with prescribed local openings, integral off a finite set, avoiding a codimension-two closed set elsewhere.
- **claim-power-approximation** (construction, [source](#stmt-prop-power-approximation)): Let $G$ be $k$-anisotropic, with $P$, $\eta$, $R$, and $X=x_*G$ as above. Fix $\gamma\in G(k)$, the rational coset $\mathcal C=x_*\gamma R\subset X(k)$, a geometrically codimension-at-least-two closed subset $D\subset X$, and a finite set $B$ containing the archimedean places, $A$, and the exceptions for fixed integral models and sufficiently small residue fields. Include in $B$ a positive-rank finite place $v_0$. At $B$ prescribe nonempty local open conditions compatible with the closure of $\mathcal C$ in Proposition <a href="#prop:finite-defect" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="prop:finite-defect">5</a>.* *At each finite place outside $B$, prescribe an allowed set $\mathcal U_v\subset X(k_v)$. Assume:* 1. *every integral point whose reduction avoids $D$ belongs to $\mathcal U_v$;* 2. *$\mathcal U_v$ contains a nonempty local open set;* 3. *single-pole configurations are tested by finitely many algebraic nonvanishing conditions over $P$, independent of $v$. Their nonemptiness is compatible with the following order of choices. First choose at least two disjoint basic lists, with their conjugator tuple in a nonempty Zariski open subset of its product of copies of $G$. For each such tuple there is a nonempty Zariski open subset of $X$ in which to choose $x$. After $x$ is fixed, for every finite number of additional factors there is a nonempty Zariski open subset of the corresponding product of copies of $G$ in which to choose their conjugators. For every tuple allowed by these successive choices, the tests restrict to a nonempty open subset of each absolute boundary hyperplane away from the others. They guarantee membership in $\mathcal U_v$ at a single-pole reduction, outside finitely many model exceptions for the fixed data.* *In the third condition the tests are imposed with all their Galois conjugates. Appending additional factors is required to preserve the tests on an existing hyperplane when the new parameters are set to $1$. Thus their insertion does not remove the previously available open subsets. The additional factors permit the local moves required by the second condition.* *Then there is $x'\in\mathcal C$ satisfying the prescribed conditions at $B$ and belonging to $\mathcal U_v$ for every finite $v\notin B$. Finitely many nonempty Zariski open conditions on the initial representative and conjugators can be included in their choice, provided they are compatible with the third condition.
  - Explanation: A representative inside a rational coset of the power subgroup meets prescribed local conditions at a finite set and lies in the allowed set elsewhere.
- **claim-cyclic-detection** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-foundations-cyclic-detection)): Let $M$ be a $k$-lattice split by a finite Galois extension $P/k$, and put $\Gamma=\mathop{\mathrm{Gal}}(P/k)$. Inflation identifies $\mathop{\mathrm{Sha}}_\omega^2(k,M)$ with $$\ker\left( H^2(\Gamma,M)\longrightarrow \prod_{C\subset\Gamma\ {\rm cyclic}}H^2(C,M) \right).$$ If this group is zero and $T$ is a $k$-torus with character lattice $M$, every everywhere locally soluble torsor under $T$ has a rational point and satisfies weak approximation.
  - Explanation: The almost-everywhere locally trivial part of the degree-two Tate–Shafarevich group is detected on cyclic subgroups.
- **claim-sign-extension** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-foundations-sign-extension)): Let $\Gamma$ be a finite group and $M$ a torsion-free $\Gamma$-lattice. Suppose that $c\in Z(\Gamma)$ has order two and acts on $M$ as multiplication by $-1$. An extension $$1\longrightarrow M\longrightarrow E \longrightarrow\Gamma\longrightarrow1$$ determines a class $\beta(E)\in H^1(\Gamma/\langle c\rangle,M/2M)$, and this class is zero if and only if the extension splits. If the restriction to the subgroup generated by an involution $r\in\Gamma$ splits, the value at the image of $r$ of every cocycle representing $\beta(E)$ lifts to an element $d_r\in M$ with $(1+r)d_r=0$.
  - Explanation: With a central sign involution, a lattice extension splits exactly when a mod-two class vanishes.
- **claim-norm-torus** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-norm-torus)): Let $L/k$ be quadratic, let $F/k$ be separable of degree $m\geq3$, and suppose that the normal closure of $F$, jointly with $L$, has Galois group $S_m\times C_2$, with its natural action on the embeddings of $F$. Set $$T=\ker\left( \mathop{\mathrm{Res}}_{F/k}\bigl(\mathop{\mathrm{Res}}^1_{LF/F}\mathbb G_m\bigr) \xrightarrow{\,N_{LF/L}\,}\mathop{\mathrm{Res}}^1_{L/k}\mathbb G_m \right).$$ This kernel is a torus. Every torsor under $T$ with points over all completions has a rational point, and its rational points are dense at any prescribed finite set of completions.
  - Explanation: The simultaneous norm torus satisfies the Hasse principle and weak approximation under the full symmetric-times-cyclic Galois hypothesis.
- **claim-color-dichotomy** (classification, [source](#stmt-prop-color-dichotomy)): Suppose that the finite group $V$ in Equation <a href="#eq:colors-defect" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:colors-defect">[eq:colors-defect]</a> is nontrivial. Then at least one of the following exists:* 1. *a homomorphism $\chi:U(k)\to C$ to a finite abelian group such that $\chi(S(k))\ne1$;* 2. *a homomorphism $\phi:U(k)\to\mathcal F$ to a finite nonabelian simple group such that $$\phi(V_0)=\mathcal F,\qquad R\subset\ker\phi.$$
  - Explanation: A nontrivial finite defect forces a finite abelian character nonzero on the special unitary group or a surjection onto a finite nonabelian simple group.
- **claim-commuting-approximation** (construction, [source](#stmt-prop-commuting-approximation)): With the preceding notation, the following statements hold.* 1. *Let $a,b\in A_0$. For every $v\in A$, suppose that $x_v,t_v\in U(k_v)$ commute and have determinants $a_v,b_v$. There exist commuting $x',t\in U(k)$ with $\det x'=a$ and $\det t=b$ whose $A$-components approximate the prescribed pair arbitrarily closely.* 2. *Let $\gamma\in V_0$ and $h\in U(k)$. There exist commuting $x',t\in U(k)$ with $$x'\in\gamma R,\qquad \det t=\det h,$$ and with $t_A$ arbitrarily close to $h_A$.
  - Explanation: Commuting local pairs with prescribed determinants are approximated by commuting rational unitary pairs with exact determinants, one element staying in a prescribed coset.
- **claim-local-abelianization** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-colors-local-abelianization)): Let $F_v$ be a nonarchimedean local field of characteristic zero, with residue field $\mathbb F_q$, and let $\mathcal D$ be a central division algebra over $F_v$ of odd degree $n\geq3$. Put $S_v=\mathop{\mathrm{SL}}_1(\mathcal D)$. Reduction induces an isomorphism $$S_v/\overline{[S_v,S_v]} \simeq T_v:=\ker\bigl(N_{\mathbb F_{q^n}/\mathbb F_q}: \mathbb F_{q^n}^\times\to\mathbb F_q^\times\bigr).$$ Moreover, $$\overline{[\mathcal D^\times,\mathcal D^\times]}=S_v.$$ If an element of $\mathcal D^\times$ has reduced-norm valuation $r$, its action on $T_v$ is $\sigma^r$, where $\sigma:z\mapsto z^{q^h}$ for some $h$ relatively prime to $n$.
  - Explanation: The abelianization of the norm-one group of a local division algebra is a finite-field norm-one torus and commutators of the full group are dense in it.
- **claim-commuting-lifts** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-colors-commuting-lifts)): If two elements of $Q$ commute, any lifts of them to $\widetilde Q$ commute.
  - Explanation: Commuting elements of the determinant-fiber group lift to commuting elements of the circle extension.
- **claim-no-finite-abelian-characters** (theorem, [source](#stmt-thm-no-characters)): Every homomorphism from $U(k)$ to a finite abelian group is trivial on $S(k)$.
  - Explanation: Every homomorphism from the full unitary group to a finite abelian group is trivial on the special unitary group.
- **claim-pair-invariance** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-chars-pair-invariance)): The function $F_d$ is invariant under independent left multiplications of its two arguments by elements of $U(k)$, and under $$\label{eq:chars-pair-move} (a,c)\longmapsto(a-cd,a-c).$$
  - Explanation: The difference function is invariant under independent unitary left multiplications and the pair move.
- **claim-pure-shears** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-chars-pure-shears)): For each block $i$, $H$ contains a nonidentity upper elementary unipotent supported only on block $i$, and a nonidentity lower elementary unipotent supported only on block $i$.
  - Explanation: The generated subgroup contains nonidentity upper and lower elementary unipotents supported on any single block.
- **claim-additive-span** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-chars-additive-span)): The additive subgroup generated by $U(k)$ is all of $D$: $$\mathbb Z U(k)=D.$$
  - Explanation: The additive subgroup generated by the rational unitary group is the whole division algebra.
- **claim-chars-difference** (theorem, [source](#stmt-prop-chars-difference)): Let $E\subset D$ be a $*$-stable maximal subfield, Galois over $L$. Let $d\in E^\times$ have $n$ distinct $L$-conjugates $t_1,\ldots,t_n$ whose set is disjoint from $\{\bar t_1,\ldots,\bar t_n\}$, where $\bar z=z^*$ on $E$. Then, whenever $b,bd\in\mathcal D$, $$\label{eq:chars-difference} f(bd)-f(b)=\chi(\bar d/d).$$
  - Explanation: For a spectrally disjoint element, the change of the character function under right multiplication is the character of the unitary ratio.
- **claim-chars-normalization** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-chars-normalization)): If $h=h^*\ne0$ has admissible signature and $s=\mathop{\mathrm{Nrd}}(h)\in k^\times$, there is $a\in D^\times$ with $$\label{eq:chars-normalized-norm} a^*a=sh,\qquad \mathop{\mathrm{Nrd}}(a)=s^{(n+1)/2}.$$ Consequently $$f^\#(b)=f(sb)\qquad(b\in\mathcal D^\#)$$ is well-defined using any $s\in k^\times$ for which $sb\in\mathcal D$. It is invariant under congruence by $D^\times$ and multiplication by $k^\times$.
  - Explanation: A Hermitian element of admissible signature can be normalized by congruence, giving a function invariant under congruence and rational scaling.
- **claim-chars-cyclic-field** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-chars-cyclic-field)): There is a $*$-stable maximal subfield $E\subset D$ which is cyclic of degree $n$ over $L$. Its norm-one torus $T=\ker(N_{E/L}:\mathop{\mathrm{Res}}_{E/L}\mathbb G_m\to\mathbb G_m)$ has weak approximation over $L$.
  - Explanation: A stable maximal subfield cyclic over the center exists and its norm-one torus has weak approximation.
- **claim-chars-step-invariance** (theorem, [source](#stmt-cor-chars-step-invariance)): If $t\in\mathcal C$ and $b,bt\in\mathcal D^\#$, then $$\label{eq:chars-step-invariance} f^\#(bt)=f^\#(b).$$
  - Explanation: Multiplication by an admissible conjugate leaves the normalized character function unchanged.
- **claim-chars-local-invariance** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-chars-local-invariance)): Let $b\in\mathcal D^\#$ have nonzero Hermitian part. For every $r\in\mathop{\mathrm{SL}}_1(D)(L)$ with $r_{A_D}\in W$ and $r_\Omega$ sufficiently close to $1$, one has $$\label{eq:chars-local-invariance} f^\#(br)=f^\#(b).$$
  - Explanation: The normalized function is unchanged under multiplication by norm-one elements near the identity at the real places and in the neighborhood at the anisotropic places.
- **claim-no-simple-colors** (theorem, [source](#stmt-thm-no-simple-colors)): There is no homomorphism $$\phi:U(k)\longrightarrow\mathcal F \quad\text{with}\quad R\subset\ker\phi,\qquad \phi(V_0)=\mathcal F,$$ where $\mathcal F$ is a finite nonabelian simple group.
  - Explanation: No homomorphism from the unitary group onto a finite nonabelian simple group can kill the power subgroup and be surjective on the defect preimage.
- **claim-finite-obstruction** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-finite-obstruction)): For every finite nonabelian simple group $\mathcal F$ there is a nonempty proper conjugacy-invariant subset $\mathcal S\subset\mathcal F$ with the following property. If $W\in\mathcal S$, $Z\notin\mathcal S$, and $WZ=ZW$, then it is impossible that $$\label{eq:palette-obstruction} ZW\in C(B)(ZW^{-1})C(BZ^2) \qquad\text{for every }B\in\mathcal F,$$ where $C(B)=C_{\mathcal F}(B)$ denotes the centralizer of $B$ in $\mathcal F$.
  - Explanation: Every finite nonabelian simple group has a nonempty proper conjugacy-invariant subset obstructing a double-coset condition.
- **claim-palette-measure** (theorem, [source](#stmt-prop-palette-measure)): There is a probability measure $\mu$ on $\mathcal C$ invariant under $T_b$ for every $b\in J(k)$ such that $$\mu\{c:c_h=a\}=\frac1{\|\mathcal F\|} \qquad(h\in\mathcal H,\ a\in\mathcal F).$$
  - Explanation: There is a translation-invariant probability measure on palettes with uniform coordinate marginals.
- **claim-palette-uniform-recurrence** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-palette-uniform-recurrence)): Let $0<\delta\leq1$.* 1. *There is an integer $r=r(\delta)\geq2$ such that, for every probability-preserving action $T$ of $\mathbb Z^2$ and every event $E$ of probability $\delta$, some $1\leq j\leq r$ satisfies $$\mathbb P(E\cap T_{(j,j^2)}^{-1}E)>0.$$* 2. *For every thick set $D_0\subset k$, there is a finite subset $F_0\subset D_0$, depending only on $D_0,k,\delta$, such that for every probability-preserving action $T$ of the discrete group $k^2$ and every event $E$ of probability $\delta$, some $s\in F_0$ satisfies $$\mathbb P(E\cap T_{(s,s^2)}^{-1}E)>0.$$
  - Explanation: Positive-measure events return along quadratic orbits with a bound depending only on the measure, with finite witnesses inside any thick set.
- **claim-finite-abelian-test** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-finite-abelian-test)): Suppose that $C\subset\mathcal F$ is an abelian subgroup of odd order $c>1$ such that $C_{\mathcal F}(t)=C$ for every $1\ne t\in C$. If $$\label{eq:finite-class-bound} \|\mathcal F\|>k(\mathcal F)c^4,$$ then the union $\mathcal S$ of the conjugates of $C\setminus\{1\}$ has the property required in Lemma <a href="#lem:finite-obstruction" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lem:finite-obstruction">26</a>.
  - Explanation: A self-centralizing odd-order abelian subgroup satisfying a class-number inequality supplies the required conjugacy-invariant set.
- **claim-finite-unitary-count** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-finite-unitary-count)): Let $p\ge3$ be an odd prime, let $H=\mathop{\mathrm{U}}_p(q)$ be the full isometry group of a nondegenerate Hermitian form over $\mathbb F_{q^2}$, and let $T\subset H$ be an irreducible torus of order $q^p+1$. If $w\in T$ has field degree $p$ over $\mathbb F_{q^2}$, then $$w\in T^h w^{-1}T^h\quad\text{for every }h\in H$$ is impossible.
  - Explanation: For an element of full field degree in an irreducible torus of a full unitary group, the double-coset containment cannot hold for all conjugates.
- **claim-outer-a** (theorem, [source](#stmt-thm-outer-a)): The Margulis–Platonov assertion holds for every simply connected absolutely almost simple group of outer type $A$ over a number field.
  - Explanation: The assertion holds for all outer type A groups over number fields, by induction on reduced degree.
- **claim-unitary-uniform-roots** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-unitary-uniform-roots)): Fix a completion $k_v$, a finite-dimensional matrix realization of $D$, and an integer $e\geq1$. There is a neighborhood of $1$ with the following property. An element in that neighborhood which belongs to one of the unitary or special unitary subgroups arising from a $*$-stable subalgebra of $D$, or from an invariant summand of its Hermitian module, has an $e$-th root in the same subgroup. The neighborhood can be chosen uniformly for all such subalgebras and summands of the bounded degrees occurring here.
  - Explanation: One neighborhood of the identity gives roots lying in the same unitary or special unitary subgroup for all relevant subalgebras.
- **claim-unitary-ternary** (theorem, [source](#stmt-prop-unitary-ternary)): If $D=M_3(L)$, then $V_0/R=1$.
  - Explanation: In the ternary split-algebra case the defect quotient is trivial.
- **claim-hermitian-quadratic-subfield** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-unitary-hermitian-quadratic)): There is a quadratic field $S_0=k(s)\subset D$, disjoint from $L$, with $s^*=s$ and $s^2=r\in k^\times$, such that the two geometric eigenspaces of $s$ have dimension $m$.
  - Explanation: In even degree there is a Hermitian quadratic subfield disjoint from the center with equal eigenspace dimensions.
- **claim-d4-main** (theorem, [source](#stmt-thm-d4)): Let $k$ be a number field and let $G$ be an absolutely almost simple simply connected $k$-group of type $D_4$, including the trialitarian forms. Every noncentral abstract normal subgroup of $G(k)$ is $G(k)$.
  - Explanation: For type D4 including trialitarian forms, every noncentral abstract normal subgroup is the whole group of rational points.
- **claim-d4-kill-involutions** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-d4-kill-involutions)): Every noncentral involution in $G(k)$ is killed by $q$.
  - Explanation: Every noncentral rational involution is trivial in the finite power quotient.
- **claim-d4-local-openings** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-d4-local-openings)): For every completion $F$ of $k$ there is a nonempty open set of regular semisimple elements $u\in G(F)$ such that inversion on $C_G(u)$ has a representative in $G(F)$.
  - Explanation: At every completion there is a nonempty open set of regular semisimple elements whose centralizer torus has a rational inverter.
- **claim-d4-one-wall** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-d4-one-wall)): Outside a fixed finite set of finite places, the following assertion holds. Let $u\in G(k_v)$ be regular semisimple and integral. If the semisimple part of its reduction lies on at most one pair of opposite root walls, then inversion on $C_G(u)$ has a rational representative.
  - Explanation: At almost all finite places an integral regular semisimple element reducing to at most one pair of opposite root walls has a rational inverter on its centralizer.
- **claim-d4-cyclic-cohomology** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-d4-cyclic-cohomology)): Let $\Gamma$ be a subgroup of $\mathop{\mathrm{Aut}}(\Phi_4)$ containing $W$, acting on $P_4$. Every class in $H^2(\Gamma,P_4)$ whose restriction to every cyclic subgroup vanishes is zero. Consequently, if a maximal $k$-torus $T$ of a simply connected group of type $D_4$ has character action containing $W$, then $\mathop{\mathrm{Sha}}^1(k,T)=0$.
  - Explanation: Classes in degree two cohomology of the weight lattice vanishing on cyclic subgroups vanish when the group contains the Weyl group.
- **claim-e6-main** (theorem, [source](#stmt-thm-e6)): Let $k$ be a number field and let $G$ be an absolutely almost simple simply connected $k$-group of type $E_6$. Every noncentral abstract normal subgroup of $G(k)$ is $G(k)$.
  - Explanation: For type E6, every noncentral abstract normal subgroup is the whole group of rational points.
- **claim-e6-root-involution** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-e6-root-involution)): The group $G(k)$ contains an involution geometrically conjugate to $\alpha^\vee(-1)$ for a root $\alpha$. Its centralizer $H$ is connected semisimple of type $A_1A_5$, and its simply connected covering has geometric form $$\label{eq:e6-centralizer-cover} \pi:\mathop{\mathrm{SL}}_2\times\mathop{\mathrm{SL}}_6\longrightarrow H_{\bar k}, \qquad \ker\pi=\langle(-I_2,-I_6)\rangle.$$
  - Explanation: There is a rational root involution whose centralizer is connected semisimple with a specified simply connected cover.
- **claim-e6-generic-d4** (construction, [source](#stmt-lem-e6-generic-d4)): There is a nonempty $k$-open subset $\mathcal U\subset G$ with the following property. For $u\in\mathcal U(k)$, set $n'=unu^{-1}$ and $t=n'n$. Then $t$ is regular semisimple, with maximal torus $T=C_G(t)$, and the roots of $T$ that conjugation by $n$ sends to their negatives generate a simply connected $k$-subgroup $D_*$ of type $D_4$. The group $D_*$ contains $n$ and $n'$ as geometrically conjugate elements, and contains $t$ in its maximal torus $T_*=D_*\cap T$, on which $n$ acts by inversion.
  - Explanation: On a nonempty open set the product of a root involution with a conjugate is regular semisimple and the inverted roots generate a simply connected D4 subgroup.
- **claim-e6-pullback** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-e6-pullback)): For $u\in\mathcal U(k)$, let $D_*$ be as in Lemma <a href="#lem:e6-generic-d4" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lem:e6-generic-d4">42</a>, and put $$J=C_{D_*}(n)=D_*\cap H, \qquad \widetilde J=\pi^{-1}(J)\subset\widetilde H.$$ Then $\widetilde J$ is connected semisimple simply connected. The variety $$\label{eq:e6-factorization-torsor} Y_u=\{(w,\widetilde q)\in D_*\times\widetilde H: w\pi(\widetilde q)=u\}$$ is a torsor under $\widetilde J$ and has points over every nonarchimedean completion of $k$.
  - Explanation: The full pullback subgroup is connected simply connected and the factorization variety is a torsor under it with points at every nonarchimedean completion.

        "anchor": "stmt-prop-power-approximation",
        "exact_quote": "Let $G$ be $k$-anisotropic, with $P$, $\\eta$, $R$, and $X=x_*G$ as above. Fix $\\gamma\\in G(k)$, the rational coset $\\mathcal C=x_*\\gamma R\\subset X(k)$, a geometrically codimension-at-least-two closed subset $D\\subset X$, and a finite set $B$ containing the archimedean places, $A$, and the exceptions for fixed integral models and sufficiently small residue fields. Include in $B$ a positive-rank finite place $v_0$. At $B$ prescribe nonempty local open conditions compatible with the closure of $\\mathcal C$ in Proposition <a href=\"#prop:finite-defect\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"prop:finite-defect\">5</a>.*\n\n*At each finite place outside $B$, prescribe an allowed set $\\mathcal U_v\\subset X(k_v)$. Assume:*\n\n1.  *every integral point whose reduction avoids $D$ belongs to $\\mathcal U_v$;*\n\n2.  *$\\mathcal U_v$ contains a nonempty local open set;*\n\n3.  *single-pole configurations are tested by finitely many algebraic nonvanishing conditions over $P$, independent of $v$. Their nonemptiness is compatible with the following order of choices. First choose at least two disjoint basic lists, with their conjugator tuple in a nonempty Zariski open subset of its product of copies of $G$. For each such tuple there is a nonempty Zariski open subset of $X$ in which to choose $x$. After $x$ is fixed, for every finite number of additional factors there is a nonempty Zariski open subset of the corresponding product of copies of $G$ in which to choose their conjugators. For every tuple allowed by these successive choices, the tests restrict to a nonempty open subset of each absolute boundary hyperplane away from the others. They guarantee membership in $\\mathcal U_v$ at a single-pole reduction, outside finitely many model exceptions for the fixed data.*\n\n*In the third condition the tests are imposed with all their Galois conjugates. Appending additional factors is required to preserve the tests on an existing hyperplane when the new parameters are set to $1$. Thus their insertion does not remove the previously available open subsets. The additional factors permit the local moves required by the second condition.*\n\n*Then there is $x'\\in\\mathcal C$ satisfying the prescribed conditions at $B$ and belonging to $\\mathcal U_v$ for every finite $v\\notin B$. Finitely many nonempty Zariski open conditions on the initial representative and conjugators can be included in their choice, provided they are compatible with the third condition.",
        "source_id": "PM-018.2"
      },

Include in $B$ a positive-rank finite place $v_0$. At $B$ prescribe nonempty local open conditions compatible with the closure of $\\mathcal C$ in Proposition <a href=\"#prop:finite-defect\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"prop:finite-defect\">5</a>.*\n\n*At each finite place outside $B$, prescribe an allowed set $\\mathcal U_v\\subset X(k_v)$.       "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-foundations-cyclic-detection",
        "exact_quote": "Let $M$ be a $k$-lattice split by a finite Galois extension $P/k$, and put $\\Gamma=\\mathop{\\mathrm{Gal}}(P/k)$. Inflation identifies $\\mathop{\\mathrm{Sha}}_\\omega^2(k,M)$ with $$\\ker\\left(\n H^2(\\Gamma,M)\\longrightarrow\n             \\prod_{C\\subset\\Gamma\\ {\\rm cyclic}}H^2(C,M)\n \\right).$$ If this group is zero and $T$ is a $k$-torus with character lattice $M$, every everywhere locally soluble torsor under $T$ has a rational point and satisfies weak approximation.",
        "source_id": "PM-018.2"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-foundations-cyclic-detection",

          "source_id": "PM-018.2"
        }

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-foundations-sign-extension",
        "exact_quote": "Let $\\Gamma$ be a finite group and $M$ a torsion-free $\\Gamma$-lattice. Suppose that $c\\in Z(\\Gamma)$ has order two and acts on $M$ as multiplication by $-1$. An extension $$1\\longrightarrow M\\longrightarrow E\n        \\longrightarrow\\Gamma\\longrightarrow1$$ determines a class $\\beta(E)\\in H^1(\\Gamma/\\langle c\\rangle,M/2M)$, and this class is zero if and only if the extension splits. If the restriction to the subgroup generated by an involution $r\\in\\Gamma$ splits, the value at the image of $r$ of every cocycle representing $\\beta(E)$ lifts to an element $d_r\\in M$ with $(1+r)d_r=0$.",
        "source_id": "PM-018.2"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-foundations-sign-extension",
          "exact_quote": "Let $\\Gamma$ be a finite group and $M$ a torsion-free $\\Gamma$-lattice. Suppose that $c\\in Z(\\Gamma)$ has order two and acts on $M$ as multiplication by $-1$. If the restriction to the subgroup generated by an involution $r\\in\\Gamma$ splits, the value at the image of $r$ of every cocycle representing $\\beta(E)$ lifts to an element $d_r\\in M$ with $(1+r)d_r=0$.",
          "source_id": "PM-018.2"
        }
      ],
      "exact_statement": "Let $\\Gamma$ be a finite group and $M$ a torsion-free $\\Gamma$-lattice. Suppose that $c\\in Z(\\Gamma)$ has order two and acts on $M$ as multiplication by $-1$.       "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-norm-torus",
        "exact_quote": "Let $L/k$ be quadratic, let $F/k$ be separable of degree $m\\geq3$, and suppose that the normal closure of $F$, jointly with $L$, has Galois group $S_m\\times C_2$, with its natural action on the embeddings of $F$. Set $$T=\\ker\\left(\n \\mathop{\\mathrm{Res}}_{F/k}\\bigl(\\mathop{\\mathrm{Res}}^1_{LF/F}\\mathbb G_m\\bigr)\n       \\xrightarrow{\\,N_{LF/L}\\,}\\mathop{\\mathrm{Res}}^1_{L/k}\\mathbb G_m\n \\right).$$ This kernel is a torus. Every torsor under $T$ with points over all completions has a rational point, and its rational points are dense at any prescribed finite set of completions.",
        "source_id": "PM-018.2"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {

Every torsor under $T$ with points over all completions has a rational point, and its rational points are dense at any prescribed finite set of completions.",
          "source_id": "PM-018.2"
        }

      "exact_statement": "Let $L/k$ be quadratic, let $F/k$ be separable of degree $m\\geq3$, and suppose that the normal closure of $F$, jointly with $L$, has Galois group $S_m\\times C_2$, with its natural action on the embeddings of $F$.         "exact_quote": "With the preceding notation, the following statements hold.*\n\n1.  *Let $a,b\\in A_0$.       "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-colors-local-abelianization",
        "exact_quote": "Let $F_v$ be a nonarchimedean local field of characteristic zero, with residue field $\\mathbb F_q$, and let $\\mathcal D$ be a central division algebra over $F_v$ of odd degree $n\\geq3$. Put $S_v=\\mathop{\\mathrm{SL}}_1(\\mathcal D)$. Reduction induces an isomorphism $$S_v/\\overline{[S_v,S_v]}\n \\simeq T_v:=\\ker\\bigl(N_{\\mathbb F_{q^n}/\\mathbb F_q}:\n                       \\mathbb F_{q^n}^\\times\\to\\mathbb F_q^\\times\\bigr).$$ Moreover, $$\\overline{[\\mathcal D^\\times,\\mathcal D^\\times]}=S_v.$$ If an element of $\\mathcal D^\\times$ has reduced-norm valuation $r$, its action on $T_v$ is $\\sigma^r$, where $\\sigma:z\\mapsto z^{q^h}$ for some $h$ relatively prime to $n$.",
        "source_id": "PM-018.2"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-colors-local-abelianization",
          "exact_quote": "Let $F_v$ be a nonarchimedean local field of characteristic zero, with residue field $\\mathbb F_q$, and let $\\mathcal D$ be a central division algebra over $F_v$ of odd degree $n\\geq3$. Reduction induces an isomorphism $$S_v/\\overline{[S_v,S_v]}\n \\simeq T_v:=\\ker\\bigl(N_{\\mathbb F_{q^n}/\\mathbb F_q}:\n                       \\mathbb F_{q^n}^\\times\\to\\mathbb F_q^\\times\\bigr).$$ Moreover, $$\\overline{[\\mathcal D^\\times,\\mathcal D^\\times]}=S_v.$$ If an element of $\\mathcal D^\\times$ has reduced-norm valuation $r$, its action on $T_v$ is $\\sigma^r$, where $\\sigma:z\\mapsto z^{q^h}$ for some $h$ relatively prime to $n$.",
          "source_id": "PM-018.2"
        }

      "exact_statement": "Let $F_v$ be a nonarchimedean local field of characteristic zero, with residue field $\\mathbb F_q$, and let $\\mathcal D$ be a central division algebra over $F_v$ of odd degree $n\\geq3$.       "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-palette-uniform-recurrence",
        "exact_quote": "Let $0<\\delta\\leq1$.*\n\n1.  *There is an integer $r=r(\\delta)\\geq2$ such that, for every probability-preserving action $T$ of $\\mathbb Z^2$ and every event $E$ of probability $\\delta$, some $1\\leq j\\leq r$ satisfies $$\\mathbb P(E\\cap T_{(j,j^2)}^{-1}E)>0.$$*\n\n2.  *For every thick set $D_0\\subset k$, there is a finite subset $F_0\\subset D_0$, depending only on $D_0,k,\\delta$, such that for every probability-preserving action $T$ of the discrete group $k^2$ and every event $E$ of probability $\\delta$, some $s\\in F_0$ satisfies $$\\mathbb P(E\\cap T_{(s,s^2)}^{-1}E)>0.$$",
        "source_id": "PM-018.2"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-palette-uniform-recurrence",
          "exact_quote": "Let $0<\\delta\\leq1$.*\n\n1.  *There is an integer $r=r(\\delta)\\geq2$ such that, for every probability-preserving action $T$ of $\\mathbb Z^2$ and every event $E$ of probability $\\delta$, some $1\\leq j\\leq r$ satisfies $$\\mathbb P(E\\cap T_{(j,j^2)}^{-1}E)>0.$$*\n\n2.  *For every thick set $D_0\\subset k$, there is a finite subset $F_0\\subset D_0$, depending only on $D_0,k,\\delta$, such that for every probability-preserving action $T$ of the discrete group $k^2$ and every event $E$ of probability $\\delta$, some $s\\in F_0$ satisfies $$\\mathbb P(E\\cap T_{(s,s^2)}^{-1}E)>0.$$",
          "source_id": "PM-018.2"
        }
      ],

<a id="pm-s2"></a>
## 2. Definitions, validity and bounds

- **anisotropic set and local product** ([source](#sec:introduction)): Let $$A=A(G,k)=\{v\text{ nonarchimedean}:\mathop{\mathrm{rank}}_{k_v}G=0\}, \qquad H_A=\prod_{v\in A}G(k_v),$$ and let $\delta_A:G(k)\to H_A$ be the diagonal homomorphism.
- **power subgroup** ([source](#pm-h-9)): For an integer $e\geq 1$, the notation $$G(k)^e=\langle g^e:g\in G(k)\rangle$$ always denotes the abstract subgroup generated by the powers.
- **assertion (MP)** ([source](#pm-h-9)): We refer to the assertion of Theorem <a href="#thm:main" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:main">1</a> for a particular group over a particular number field as *(MP)*.
- **finite defect quotient** ([source](#stmt-prop-finite-defect)): Fix $e\geq1$, and put $$R=G(k)^e,\qquad P_0=\overline{\delta(R)}\subset G_A,\qquad V_0=\delta^{-1}(P_0),\qquad V=V_0/R.$$
- **single-pole reduction** ([source](#eq:foundations-single-pole)): We call a reduction a *single-pole reduction* if exactly one of these absolute coordinates vanishes.
- **lattice** ([source](#pm-h-13)): A *lattice* is a finitely generated free abelian group with a continuous Galois action, which necessarily has finite image.
- **admissible set D-sharp** ([source](#pm-h-29)): Define $\mathcal D^\#$ to consist of the nonzero skew elements and the $b\in D$ for which $h=b+b^*\ne0$ has admissible signature.
- **palette** ([source](#pm-h-33)): We call this configuration a *palette*, and let $\mathcal C\subset\mathcal F^{\mathcal H}$ be the closure of the rational palettes.
- **thick set** ([source](#pm-h-40)): A subset $D_0$ of the additive group $k$ is called *thick* if it contains a translate of every finite subset of $k$.
- **unitary groups S and U** ([source](#sec:colors)): Write $$S=\mathop{\mathrm{SU}}(D,*),\qquad U=\mathop{\mathrm{U}}(D,*),\qquad L^1=\{a\in L^\times:a\bar a=1\},$$ where the bar is the nontrivial automorphism of $L/k$.

| id | quantity | relation | value | regime |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| q-index-finite | index of a noncentral normal subgroup in an arithmetic group | < | $\infty$ | normal subgroup noncentral |
| q-sieve-codimension | geometric codimension of the avoided closed set | ≥ | $2$ | closed subset to be avoided along a line |
| q-norm-torus-degree | degree of the separable extension | ≥ | $3$ | joint Galois group symmetric times cyclic |
| q-odd-degree | reduced degree of the division algebra | ≥ | $3$ | odd degree |
| q-torus-order | order of the residue norm-one torus | = | $(q^n-1)/(q-1)$ | residue field of the local field |
| q-weight-sum-convergence | weighted sum over rational points of the flag variety | < | $\infty$ | exponent greater than the stated threshold |
| q-recurrence-event-probability | probability of the simultaneous recurrence event | = | $\|\mathcal F\|^{-\|\mathcal F\|}$ | uniform coordinate marginals |
| q-class-bound | order of the finite simple group | > | $k(\mathcal F)c^4$ | subgroup of odd order |
| q-exceptional-class-number | number of conjugacy classes of an exceptional finite group of Lie type | ≤ | $100q^l$ | absolute rank l and field size q |
| q-normalized-reduced-norm | reduced norm of the normalizing element | = | $s^{(n+1)/2}$ | Hermitian element of admissible signature |

<a id="pm-s3"></a>
## 3. Proof roadmap

- **step-hasse** [claim](#stmt-thm-foundations-hasse) / [proof](#stmt-thm-foundations-hasse): Cite the Kneser–Harder–Chernousov Hasse principle and strong approximation. -> Hasse principle and weak approximation for torsors
- **step-known-mp** [claim](#stmt-thm-known-mp) / [proof](#stmt-thm-known-mp): Recall previously established cases from the literature. -> assertion known outside the remaining anisotropic types
- **step-finite-defect** [claim](#stmt-prop-finite-defect) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-finite-defect): Apply the lattice theorem and the arithmetic normal subgroup theorem, then strong approximation to the closure. -> finite index and finite defect quotient
- **step-restriction-mp** [claim](#stmt-lem-restriction-mp) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-restriction-mp): Apply the known assertion to the kernel of the restricted homomorphism factor by factor. -> continuous extension over local factors
- **step-affine-sieve** [claim](#stmt-lem-foundations-affine-sieve) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-foundations-affine-sieve): Finite projection along the direction and additive strong approximation, then bounded fiber counting. -> point avoiding the closed set at all remaining finite places
- **step-power-approximation** [claim](#stmt-prop-power-approximation) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-power-approximation): Dominant parameter lists, choice of initial point, then the line sieve with scalar invariance at the omitted place. -> representative in the coset with local conditions
- **step-cyclic-detection** [claim](#stmt-lem-foundations-cyclic-detection) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-foundations-cyclic-detection): Chebotarev density and inflation–restriction, then torus duality. -> cyclic criterion for the Tate–Shafarevich group
- **step-sign-extension** [claim](#stmt-lem-foundations-sign-extension) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-foundations-sign-extension): Compare lifts to build a cocycle modulo two and show it controls splitting. -> splitting criterion
- **step-norm-torus** [claim](#stmt-lem-norm-torus) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-norm-torus): Coxeter presentation argument for the symmetric group, with a separate case for the smallest even degree. -> Hasse principle and weak approximation for the norm torus
- **step-commuting-approximation** [claim](#stmt-prop-commuting-approximation) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-commuting-approximation): Choose a regular first element by power-factor approximation, then solve a norm torsor for the commuting second element. -> commuting rational pair with exact determinants
- **step-local-abelianization** [claim](#stmt-lem-colors-local-abelianization) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-colors-local-abelianization): Filtration of the norm-one group and commutator computation on graded pieces. -> abelianization and commutator closure
- **step-commuting-lifts** [claim](#stmt-lem-colors-commuting-lifts) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-colors-commuting-lifts): Approximate by commuting rational pairs and pass commutators to the limit. -> commuting lifts
- **step-color-dichotomy** [claim](#stmt-prop-color-dichotomy) / [proof](#pm-h-25): Class preservation, a theorem on class-preserving automorphisms of simple groups, and a metaplectic splitting argument. -> abelian character or simple quotient
- **step-pair-invariance** [claim](#stmt-lem-chars-pair-invariance) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-chars-pair-invariance): Direct expansion in the noncommutative algebra. -> invariance of the difference function
- **step-pure-shears** [claim](#stmt-lem-chars-pure-shears) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-chars-pure-shears): Isolate one block by degree-two slope functions, then Zariski density and commutators. -> single-block elementary unipotents
- **step-additive-span** [claim](#stmt-lem-chars-additive-span) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-chars-additive-span): Local additive closure at each prime, then clearing denominators. -> additive generation of the algebra
- **step-chars-difference** [claim](#stmt-prop-chars-difference) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-chars-difference): Transitivity of the invariance group on nonzero pairs, then evaluation at a specific pair. -> difference formula
- **step-chars-normalization** [claim](#stmt-lem-chars-normalization) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-chars-normalization): Local solutions by Hermitian classification, then the Hasse principle for the torsor. -> normalized function
- **step-chars-cyclic-field** [claim](#stmt-lem-chars-cyclic-field) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-chars-cyclic-field): Grunwald–Wang cyclic extension, Skolem–Noether embedding and Hilbert 90. -> stable cyclic maximal subfield
- **step-chars-step-invariance** [claim](#stmt-cor-chars-step-invariance) / [proof](#proof-stmt-cor-chars-step-invariance): Conjugate to a standard element and use annihilation by the exponent. -> step invariance
- **step-chars-local-invariance** [claim](#stmt-lem-chars-local-invariance) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-chars-local-invariance): Path factorization into torus loops and a fine mesh of admissible rational steps. -> local invariance near the identity
- **step-no-characters** [claim](#stmt-thm-no-characters) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-chars-local-invariance-2): Continuity on the special unitary group, extension to local factors, then commutators. -> trivial character on the special unitary group
- **step-finite-abelian-test** [claim](#stmt-lem-finite-abelian-test) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-finite-abelian-test): Counting with the Frobenius–Schur formula and Cauchy–Schwarz. -> obstruction set
- **step-finite-unitary-count** [claim](#stmt-lem-finite-unitary-count) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-finite-unitary-count): Compare class size with involution centralizer bounds in the unitary group. -> impossibility of the double-coset containment
- **step-finite-obstruction** [claim](#stmt-lem-finite-obstruction) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-finite-unitary-count-2): Case analysis through the classification of finite simple groups. -> obstruction set for every finite nonabelian simple group
- **step-palette-measure** [claim](#stmt-prop-palette-measure) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-palette-measure): Conformal law from Eisenstein series, Howe–Moore decay for uniform marginals, then Følner averaging. -> invariant measure with uniform marginals
- **step-palette-recurrence** [claim](#stmt-lem-palette-uniform-recurrence) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-palette-uniform-recurrence): Spectral measures and Weyl equidistribution on a limit measure. -> uniform return witnesses
- **step-no-simple-colors** [claim](#stmt-thm-no-simple-colors) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-palette-uniform-recurrence-2): Recurrence forces the obstruction condition, then invariance under squares forces constancy of the indicator. -> contradiction
- **step-unitary-uniform-roots** [claim](#stmt-lem-unitary-uniform-roots) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-unitary-uniform-roots): Binomial power series near the identity. -> uniform roots
- **step-unitary-ternary** [claim](#stmt-prop-unitary-ternary) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-unitary-ternary): Three binary transformations and norm equations on a Châtelet surface. -> trivial defect
- **step-hermitian-quadratic** [claim](#stmt-lem-unitary-hermitian-quadratic) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-unitary-hermitian-quadratic): Hasse principle for homogeneous spaces with connected stabilizer. -> quadratic subfield
- **step-outer-a** [claim](#stmt-thm-outer-a) / [proof](#stmt-thm-outer-a): Induction on reduced degree combining all unitary cases. -> assertion for outer type A
- **step-d4-kill-involutions** [claim](#stmt-lem-d4-kill-involutions) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-d4-kill-involutions): Embed the involution in a rank-two subgroup where the assertion is known. -> involutions killed
- **step-d4-local-openings** [claim](#stmt-lem-d4-local-openings) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-d4-local-openings): Existence of a rational involution by Borovoi, then a submersion argument. -> local open sets with rational inversion
- **step-d4-one-wall** [claim](#stmt-lem-d4-one-wall) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-d4-one-wall): Unramified centralizer model and inversion in rank-one factors. -> rational inversion at good places
- **step-d4-cyclic-cohomology** [claim](#stmt-lem-d4-cyclic-cohomology) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-d4-cyclic-cohomology): Apply the sign-extension criterion and show absence of invariants modulo two. -> vanishing of the first Tate–Shafarevich group
- **step-d4-main** [claim](#stmt-thm-d4) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-d4-cyclic-cohomology-2): Write each coset representative as a product of two noncentral involutions. -> trivial power quotient
- **step-e6-root-involution** [claim](#stmt-lem-e6-root-involution) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-e6-root-involution): Root-lattice computation and Borovoi's theorem with a real-place check. -> rational root involution
- **step-e6-generic-d4** [claim](#stmt-lem-e6-generic-d4) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-e6-generic-d4): Dimension count of a morphism into the conjugacy class. -> open set with D4 subgroup
- **step-e6-pullback** [claim](#stmt-lem-e6-pullback) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-e6-pullback): Identify the full inverse image and show the factorization variety is a torsor. -> torsor with local points
- **step-e6-main** [claim](#stmt-thm-e6) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-e6-pullback-2): Factor a representative through the D4 subgroup and the centralizer cover using the Hasse principle. -> trivial power quotient
- **step-main-mp** [claim](#stmt-thm-main) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-e6-pullback-3): Combine all cases with the finite-defect reduction and density of the diagonal. -> open normal subgroup description
- **step-positive-density-csp** [claim](#stmt-cor-positive-density-csp) / [proof](#proof-stmt-cor-positive-density-csp): Verify that the main theorem supplies the hypothesis of the cited positive-density theorem. -> trivial congruence kernel

<a id="pm-s4"></a>
## 4. Engineering Triptych

### Panel 1 - epistemic contract

- **inv-noncentral-normal-subgroup-description**: For an absolutely almost simple simply connected group over a number field, every normal subgroup of the rational points that is not contained in the center is the preimage under the diagonal map of an open normal subgroup of the product of the anisotropic nonarchimedean local groups. When that set of places is empty the subgroup is the whole group.
- **inv-finite-index-of-noncentral-normal**: Within the standing setting, every abstract noncentral normal subgroup of the rational points has finite index, so the problem reduces to the finite quotient that approximation does not detect.

### Panel 2 - negative knowledge

- **anti-other-base-fields**: Apply the classification of normal subgroups to groups over fields that are not number fields, such as function fields. -> The proof relies on number-field tools (strong approximation, arithmetic lattices, Hasse principle for number fields), so the conclusion is not established outside that setting.
- **anti-central-subgroups**: Describe a central normal subgroup of the rational points as the preimage of an open normal subgroup of the local product. -> The statement excludes central subgroups, so the preimage description is not asserted for them.
- **anti-congruence-outside-density-range**: Use the corollary to conclude triviality of the congruence kernel for arbitrary finite or infinite sets of places. -> The corollary only covers sets of places meeting the completely split places with positive density; other ranges are not addressed.

### Panel 3 - reference implementation and oracle

Applicability: `not_applicable`. The paper's results are existence and classification statements about abstract normal subgroups of the rational points of an algebraic group over a number field, an infinite group. The proof proceeds through approximation, Hasse principle, recurrence for measures and the classification of finite simple groups, none of which gives a finite procedure whose output a program could compute and check against an independent oracle. The main theorem is the assertion that every noncentral normal subgroup equals the preimage of an open normal subgroup; no explicit algorithm or bound is produced.

## 5. Domain engineering contracts

| domain | applies | strength | maturity | basis | why |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| software | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The paper proves a structural classification of abstract normal subgroups of rational points of simple algebraic groups. It states no executable procedure or interface, so a library cannot be derived from it. |
| games_simulation | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The objects are groups of rational points over number fields and finite local quotients. They have no state space, dynamics or discretisation that a simulation could use. |
| tools | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | A tool would need a computable input and output. Here the conclusion is existential and the subgroups N and W are abstract, with no finite description of N given. |
| algorithms | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | No algorithm with explicit steps is stated. The proofs use existence arguments such as approximation, Hasse principles, and recurrence along measures, so no running time or output can be specified. |
| data_structures | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The source defines no representation or invariant-maintaining structure for elements of G(k). The groups involved are infinite and are handled only abstractly. |
| hardware_accelerators | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | There is no numerical kernel, fixed-width arithmetic or parallel workload in the source. Its content is arithmetic-geometric and group-theoretic existence. |
| crypto_security | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The paper gives no hardness assumption, protocol, or primitive. A structural simplicity statement for rational points does not yield a security guarantee. |
| machine_learning | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | There is no data, loss, learning rule or statistical model in the source. The probability measures it builds on color palettes are proof devices, not models. |
| optimisation_operations_research | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | No objective, constraint set or optimisation problem is posed. The source only decides which subgroups can be normal. |
| distributed_systems | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The local-to-global structure over places of a number field is purely mathematical. It involves no nodes, messages, failures or consensus. |
| other | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The principal value is internal to pure mathematics, as an input to the congruence subgroup problem. It gives no engineering contract beyond the stated positive-density corollary. |

<a id="pm-s6"></a>
## 6. Applicability limits and search

- **L-number-field-simple-group** (hypothesis): The classification is proved only for an absolutely almost simple, simply connected algebraic group over a number field. Nothing in the source extends it to other base fields, to reductive or non-simply-connected groups, or to non-simple groups. Blocked use: ['Applying the open-normal-subgroup description to groups over global function fields or other non-number fields', 'Applying it to general linear, adjoint or other reductive groups, or to non-simply-connected forms']
- **L-noncentral-subgroup** (hypothesis): The description of a normal subgroup as the preimage of an open normal subgroup of the anisotropic local product is asserted only for subgroups not contained in the center. Blocked use: ['Describing a central normal subgroup of the rational points as the preimage of an open normal subgroup', 'Using the theorem to say anything about the center itself']
- **L-csp-density-range** (formal_scope): Triviality of the congruence kernel is derived only when the place set contains the archimedean places, avoids anisotropic nonarchimedean places, and meets the completely split places with positive Dirichlet density, the density being assumed to exist. Blocked use: ['Concluding the classical congruence subgroup property for an arbitrary finite set of places', 'Concluding triviality of the kernel for an infinite set of places without the positive density condition']
- **L-csp-outside-range** (counterexample_scope): The source explicitly declines to address the finite-place-set conjecture, arbitrary infinite place sets, or centrality or finiteness of congruence kernels outside the positive-density range. Blocked use: ['Citing the corollary as a proof of centrality or finiteness of the congruence kernel for sets outside the stated range', 'Citing the corollary as evidence for the finite-set conjecture']
- **L-power-subgroup-abstract** (formal_scope): The finite defect and the reduction to its triviality concern the abstract subgroup generated by powers, taken without closure. The closure only describes the local image and does not by itself recover the subgroup. Blocked use: ['Replacing the abstract power subgroup by its topological closure when arguing that the defect vanishes', 'Reading finite index of a noncentral normal subgroup as equality with a preimage of an open subgroup']
- **L-single-pole-hypothesis** (approximation): The power-factor approximation requires a substantive hypothesis on single-pole configurations, to be verified separately in each application. It permits poles only at places that split completely in the splitting field and does not make torus parameters integral units everywhere. Blocked use: ['Applying the approximation to a coset without checking the single-pole tests and their compatible order of choices', 'Assuming the representative has integral unit torus parameters at every finite place']
- **L-anisotropic-division-odd-degree** (edge_case): The abelian-character exclusion is proved for a central division algebra of odd degree at least three with a unitary involution over a quadratic extension. Even degree, split or matrix algebras, and degree two are handled by separate arguments or earlier cases. Blocked use: ['Applying the character-vanishing theorem to a unitary group of even degree or of a non-division algebra', 'Applying it to degree one or to degree two']
- **L-metaplectic-exclusion** (edge_case): The metaplectic injectivity used to split the circle extension is stated for simply connected absolutely almost simple groups not of type A_1, with a set of anisotropic nonarchimedean places. Quotients of rank-one type fall outside it. Blocked use: ['Invoking the injectivity of the restriction map for a group of type A_1', 'Invoking it at places that are archimedean or where the group is isotropic']
- **L-d4-anisotropic-reduction** (hypothesis): The type D4 and E6 arguments assume the group is anisotropic over the number field, relying on the isotropic case being previously established. Within the anisotropic case there are no anisotropic nonarchimedean factors, so the conclusion is that every noncentral normal subgroup is the whole group. Blocked use: ['Transferring the D4 statement to a group over a base field other than a number field', 'Using the D4 or E6 argument for an isotropic group without the earlier known cases']
- **L-nonconstructive-palette-measure** (nonconstructive): The translation-invariant measure with uniform marginals is obtained as a weak limit by compactness and averaging. It is not given by an explicit formula, so no procedure is supplied that computes it. Blocked use: ['Presenting the measure as a computable object or sampling procedure', 'Deriving an explicit bound on recurrence times from its construction']
- **L-nonconstructive-finite-obstruction** (nonconstructive): The obstruction subset in a finite nonabelian simple group is shown to exist by a case analysis through the classification, with a different subset chosen in each family. The statement gives no uniform description of the subset. Blocked use: ['Treating the obstruction subset as a canonical, uniformly defined set across all simple groups', 'Using the lemma as a computational certificate without the family-specific data']
- **L-no-effective-bounds** (unknown): The source gives no explicit bound for the exponent, the finite set of exceptional places, or the size of the finite defect quotient. Effectivity of the constructions is therefore not claimed. Blocked use: ['Quoting a numerical bound on the index of a noncentral normal subgroup', 'Claiming an algorithm that finds the open normal subgroup from the given subgroup']

<a id="pm-s7"></a>
## 7. Complete mathematical paper


We prove the Margulis–Platonov conjecture over number fields. For an absolutely almost simple simply connected group, every noncentral abstract normal subgroup of its rational points is the inverse image of an open normal subgroup of the product of its anisotropic nonarchimedean local groups.

<a id="sec:introduction"></a>
### Introduction

Let $k$ be a number field and let $G$ be an absolutely almost simple simply connected algebraic $k$-group. For a place $v$ of $k$, write $k_v$ for the completion. The local group $G(k_v)$ has its usual topology. Let $$A=A(G,k)=\{v\text{ nonarchimedean}:\mathop{\mathrm{rank}}_{k_v}G=0\},
 \qquad H_A=\prod_{v\in A}G(k_v),$$ and let $\delta_A:G(k)\to H_A$ be the diagonal homomorphism. The set $A$ is finite. The product $H_A$ is given its product topology; if $A$ is empty, it is the trivial group.

The Margulis–Platonov conjecture asserts that the noncentral normal subgroups of $G(k)$ are accounted for by this finite collection of compact local groups. Normal subgroups in this assertion are abstract: no topology or finite-generation condition is imposed on them. We prove the conjecture in this form.

<a id="stmt-thm-main"></a>
<div id="thm:main" class="theorem">

**Theorem 1**. *Let $k$ be a number field and $G$ an absolutely almost simple simply connected algebraic $k$-group. If $N\triangleleft G(k)$ is not contained in $Z(G(k))$, then there is an open normal subgroup $W\triangleleft H_A$ such that $$N=\delta_A^{-1}(W).$$*

</div>

Thus the result resolves the Margulis–Platonov conjecture positively over number fields, including globally anisotropic groups. In the case $A=\varnothing$, the conclusion is $N=G(k)$, not merely that $N$ has finite index or is dense in a local topology.

<a id="pm-h-3"></a>
#### Background and significance

The problem joins two kinds of rigidity. At isotropic completions, generation by unipotent subgroups and simplicity modulo the center leave little room for normal quotients. At anisotropic nonarchimedean completions, compactness allows finite quotients and their open kernels. The assertion is that there is no additional abstract normal-subgroup obstruction invisible to these local factors.

The conjecture grew from Kneser’s quaternionic simplicity question and Platonov’s local-to-global simplicity proposal. Margulis formulated the description by open subgroups of the anisotropic finite local product <a id="pm-cite-1"></a>[[Margulis1979](#ref-Margulis1979), Russian original, Section 2.4.8]. Strong approximation and Margulis’s normal subgroup theorem supply the arithmetic framework and finite-index reduction <a id="pm-cite-2"></a>[[PRbook](#ref-PRbook), [Margulis](#ref-Margulis)]. The isotropic case follows from the Kneser–Tits theorem over global fields; Gille’s account includes the final outer-$E_6$ cases, using work of Garibaldi and Chernousov–Timoshenko <a id="pm-cite-3"></a>[[Gille2009](#ref-Gille2009), Theorem 8.1]. For anisotropic groups, Chernousov proved projective simplicity of the rational points when the absolute rank is at least two and the group splits over a quadratic extension <a id="pm-cite-4"></a>[[Chernousov1990](#ref-Chernousov1990), Theorem 1]. Tomanov and Sury established substantial classical and low-index unitary cases <a id="pm-cite-5"></a>[[Tomanov1990](#ref-Tomanov1990), [Sury1991](#ref-Sury1991)].

For inner type $A$, the arithmetic work of Platonov–Rapinchuk and Raghunathan <a id="pm-cite-6"></a>[[PR1984](#ref-PR1984), [Raghunathan1988](#ref-Raghunathan1988)] preceded the reduction of Rapinchuk–Potapchik to finite simple quotients of the multiplicative group of a division algebra <a id="pm-cite-7"></a>[[RapinchukPotapchik1996](#ref-RapinchukPotapchik1996)]. Segev obtained a commuting-graph obstruction, and Segev–Seitz verified the required finite-simple-group properties, completing this case <a id="pm-cite-8"></a>[[Segev1999](#ref-Segev1999), [SegevSeitz2002](#ref-SegevSeitz2002)]. Rapinchuk–Segev–Seitz later proved that every finite quotient of the multiplicative group of a finite-dimensional division algebra is solvable <a id="pm-cite-9"></a>[[RapinchukSegevSeitz2002](#ref-RapinchukSegevSeitz2002)]; Rapinchuk gave a shorter proof of the inner-$A$ case using two-generation of finite simple groups <a id="pm-cite-10"></a>[[Rapinchuk2006](#ref-Rapinchuk2006)]. The established cases are surveyed in <a id="pm-cite-11"></a>[[PRsurvey](#ref-PRsurvey), Sections 3.4–3.5]. They include isotropic groups and all inner forms of type $A$, as well as the other types outside the remaining anisotropic outer $A$, trialitarian $D_4$, and $E_6$ cases. We use that reduction in Theorem <a href="#thm:known-mp" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:known-mp">3</a> and supply the remaining arguments here. In particular, the previously established inner-type-$A$ theorem is available over every number field that occurs in a restriction-of-scalars subgroup.

The conjecture also enters the congruence subgroup problem: it is a normal-subgroup input to several descriptions of congruence kernels. That relationship does not justify using a theorem which assumes the conjecture to prove it. Our central-extension argument instead uses the unconditional finite-nonarchimedean injectivity in the metaplectic-kernel computation of Prasad and Rapinchuk <a id="pm-cite-12"></a>[[PRmetaplectic](#ref-PRmetaplectic), Proposition 2.6]. The distinction between metaplectic vanishing and congruence-kernel conclusions under the normal-subgroup hypothesis is also explicit in <a id="pm-cite-13"></a>[[Rapinchuk2026](#ref-Rapinchuk2026), Section 2].

<a id="pm-h-4"></a>
#### A positive-density congruence consequence

Theorem <a href="#thm:main" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:main">1</a> supplies the Margulis–Platonov hypothesis in Rapinchuk’s positive-density theorem, giving the following consequence.

<a id="stmt-cor-positive-density-csp"></a>
<div id="cor:positive-density-csp" class="corollary">

**Corollary 2** (Positive-density congruence subgroup property). *Let $k$ be a number field and $G$ an absolutely almost simple simply connected algebraic $k$-group. Let $M/k$ be the minimal Galois extension over which $G$ becomes an inner form of the split group, and let $\operatorname{Spl}(M/k)$ be the set of nonarchimedean places of $k$ that split completely in $M$. Suppose that $S$ is a set of places of $k$ which contains all archimedean places, contains no nonarchimedean place $v$ with $\mathop{\mathrm{rank}}_{k_v}G=0$, and satisfies $$d_k\bigl(S\cap\operatorname{Spl}(M/k)\bigr)>0,$$ where $d_k$ denotes Dirichlet density and the indicated density is assumed to exist. Then the $S$-congruence kernel is trivial: $$C^S(G)=\{1\}.$$ Equivalently, for any fixed faithful $k$-defined representation of $G$, the associated group $G(\mathcal O(S))$ over the ring of $S$-integers has the classical congruence subgroup property: every finite-index normal subgroup contains a principal congruence subgroup of some nonzero ideal level.*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-cor-positive-density-csp"><summary>Proof of stmt-cor-positive-density-csp</summary>

*Proof.* Theorem <a href="#thm:main" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:main">1</a> is the Margulis–Platonov statement assumed in <a id="pm-cite-14"></a>[[Rapinchuk2026](#ref-Rapinchuk2026), Theorem 1.1], for the same group and number field. The other hypotheses are exactly those imposed above, so that theorem gives $C^S(G)=\{1\}$. The equivalence with the classical congruence subgroup property is recalled in <a id="pm-cite-15"></a>[[Rapinchuk2026](#ref-Rapinchuk2026), Section 2]. ◻

</details>

For an inner form of the split group, $M=k$, and the density condition is positive Dirichlet density of the nonarchimedean places in $S$. The corollary is restricted to the stated positive-density range: it does not address Serre’s finite-$S$ conjecture, arbitrary infinite $S$, or centrality or finiteness of congruence kernels outside that range.

<a id="pm-h-5"></a>
#### The finite obstruction

We first prove that a noncentral normal subgroup $N$ has finite index. Choose a positive integer $e$ that annihilates the finite group $G(k)/N$, and let $$R=G(k)^e=\langle g^e:g\in G(k)\rangle,\qquad
 P_0=\overline{\delta_A(R)},\qquad
 V_0=\delta_A^{-1}(P_0).$$ Here $G(k)^e$ is the subgroup generated by powers, not just the set of powers. The group $P_0$ is open and normal in $H_A$. It remains to show that the finite group $V_0/R$ is trivial. This is the distinction between the desired abstract equality and the closure statement furnished by approximation.

Section <a href="#sec:foundations" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:foundations">2</a> develops two tools for this finite obstruction. First, it constructs representatives of a fixed coset modulo $R$ satisfying specified local openings and avoiding a prescribed codimension-two reduction locus at almost all other places. The parameters are products of conjugates of torus powers. Scalar invariance permits an affine sieve while retaining control at the omitted approximation place; possible single poles are treated explicitly. Second, a signed permutation lattice calculation gives the Hasse principle and weak approximation for the norm torus needed to prescribe commuting elements with exact determinants.

<a id="pm-h-6"></a>
#### Odd-degree division algebras

The first unresolved case is a special unitary group $S=\mathop{\mathrm{SU}}(D,*)$, where $D$ is an odd-degree central division algebra over a quadratic extension $L/k$ and $*$ is a unitary involution. Let $U=\mathop{\mathrm{U}}(D,*)$ be its full unitary group. Section <a href="#sec:colors" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:colors">3</a> proves that a nontrivial finite defect for $S(k)$ yields one of two objects on $U(k)$: a finite abelian character nonzero on $S(k)$, or a homomorphism to a finite nonabelian simple group which is surjective on the defect preimage $V_0$. The commuting approximation and central-extension arguments are needed to obtain this dichotomy on the full unitary group. The class-preserving extension step adapts the inner-type argument of Rapinchuk–Potapchik <a id="pm-cite-16"></a>[[RapinchukPotapchik1996](#ref-RapinchukPotapchik1996), Section 2]; here the commuting approximation must also prescribe determinants. A quotient of a unitary subgroup need not extend to the multiplicative group of its algebra.

Section <a href="#sec:characters" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:characters">4</a> rules out the first object. An algebraic difference function on $D$ has enough exact elementary invariances to be constant on the required orbits. The exact additive span of $U(k)$ in $D$, together with the known inner-type-$A$ theorem and a real-signature interpolation argument, forces any finite abelian character to vanish on $S(k)$.

Sections <a href="#sec:palettes" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:palettes">5</a> and <a href="#sec:finite-groups" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:finite-groups">6</a> rule out the second object. A finite simple quotient assigns a color to each rational unitary element. The distribution of these colors is not assumed continuous. We construct a translation-invariant probability law on their finite patterns whose individual color distributions are uniform. Fractional unitary transformations turn additive recurrence into a centralizer double-coset condition. A finite-simple-group lemma, stated in Section <a href="#sec:palettes" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:palettes">5</a> and proved in Section <a href="#sec:finite-groups" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:finite-groups">6</a>, excludes that condition. Elementary transformations then force an impossible invariance under all left color translations.

The construction of the uniform-marginal law is an important part of the proof: additive averaging by itself need not preserve all colors. Conformal weights on rational points of a flag variety use Langlands’s Eisenstein-series convergence theorem <a id="pm-cite-17"></a>[[Langlands](#ref-Langlands)]. Finite-volume harmonicity and Howe–Moore matrix-coefficient decay <a id="pm-cite-18"></a>[[HoweMoore](#ref-HoweMoore), [Ciobotaru](#ref-Ciobotaru)] then provide the required marginals before additive averaging is performed. The later positive-density argument uses the multidimensional theorem of Furstenberg–Katznelson <a id="pm-cite-19"></a>[[FurstenbergKatznelson](#ref-FurstenbergKatznelson)]. The uniform-marginal construction, the coset-preserving sieve, and the explicit norm-torus calculation are developed before the final case reductions so that their hypotheses and conclusions remain visible.

<a id="pm-h-7"></a>
#### Completion of the proof

Section <a href="#sec:unitary-induction" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:unitary-induction">7</a> proves the other outer-type-$A$ cases by induction on reduced degree, simultaneously over all number fields. The ternary split-algebra case uses quadratic norm equations on a Châtelet surface. Even degree is reduced by a quaternion factorization and a smaller unitary centralizer, with a separate degree-four determinant correction. The approximation construction controls the finite places of the subgroups as they vary.

Section <a href="#sec:d4" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:d4">8</a> treats $D_4$ by finding, in each relevant rational coset, a regular element whose torus admits a rational inverter. It is a product of two involutions, each already controlled inside a type-$A_2$ subgroup. Section <a href="#sec:e6" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:e6">9</a> reduces a generic $E_6$ element to a simply connected $D_4$ subgroup and the cover of a root-involution centralizer of type $A_1A_5$. The last step is a torsor under a connected simply connected group, so its local factorizations can be globalized. The proof ends by returning to the original abstract subgroup $N$.

<a id="sec:foundations"></a>
### Finite quotients and approximation

The first objective is to isolate what local approximation does not already prove. Every noncentral normal subgroup has finite index, but its closure at the anisotropic finite places need not a priori recover the subgroup. We express this possible discrepancy as a finite quotient. We then develop two tools for eliminating it: an approximation procedure that retains the abstract rational coset, and a Hasse principle with weak approximation for a particular norm torus.

<a id="pm-h-9"></a>
#### Conventions and established inputs

Throughout, $k$ is a number field, $V_k$ is its set of places, and $G$ is an absolutely almost simple simply connected $k$-group. Write $$A=\{v\in V_k:\ v\text{ is nonarchimedean and }
                    \mathop{\mathrm{rank}}_{k_v}G=0\},\qquad
 G_A=\prod_{v\in A}G(k_v).$$ The diagonal homomorphism to this product is denoted by $\delta$. Thus $G_A=H_A$ and $\delta=\delta_A$ in the notation of the introduction. An empty product is the trivial group. The set $A$ is finite: outside finitely many places the group has a reductive model and is unramified and quasi-split, hence has positive relative rank. At a nonarchimedean place an absolutely simple anisotropic group has type $A$.

For a finite set of places $B$, a subscript $B$ on a rational element denotes its tuple of local images. Products over finitely many places have their ordinary product topology. In an adelic product we use the restricted product with respect to fixed integral compact open subgroups outside a finite set. Weak approximation means density at finitely many completions; strong approximation means density in the specified restricted adelic product.

For an integer $e\geq 1$, the notation $$G(k)^e=\langle g^e:g\in G(k)\rangle$$ always denotes the abstract subgroup generated by the powers. No closure is included in this definition. Degrees of central simple algebras are reduced degrees; for example, $\mathop{\mathrm{SL}}_1(M_r(\Delta))$ has type $A_{r\deg\Delta-1}$. Restrictions of scalars will be treated over their defining extension fields when applying an induction or an approximation theorem.

We use the strong approximation theorem for simply connected absolutely almost simple groups: if $G(k_w)$ is noncompact, then $G(k)$ is dense in the adelic product away from $w$ <a id="pm-cite-20"></a>[[PRbook](#ref-PRbook), Theorem 7.12]. We also use the lattice theorem for semisimple $S$-arithmetic groups <a id="pm-cite-21"></a>[[PRbook](#ref-PRbook), Theorem 5.7(1)], the normal subgroup theorem for irreducible $S$-arithmetic lattices of total rank at least two, and the local Kneser–Tits and simplicity theorems. In the last assertion, for a nonarchimedean $v$ with positive rank, $G(k_v)$ is perfect and $G(k_v)/Z(G(k_v))$ is abstractly simple. These results are used in their number-field forms <a id="pm-cite-22"></a>[[PRbook](#ref-PRbook), Chapters VII and IX]<a id="pm-cite-23"></a>[[Margulis](#ref-Margulis), Chapter VIII, Theorem A].

We refer to the assertion of Theorem <a href="#thm:main" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:main">1</a> for a particular group over a particular number field as *(MP)*. We require the following established cases.

<a id="stmt-thm-known-mp"></a>
<div id="thm:known-mp" class="theorem">

**Theorem 3** (Known cases of (MP)). *The assertion (MP) holds except possibly for anisotropic groups of outer type $A$, trialitarian type $D_4$, and type $E_6$. In particular it holds for every inner form of $\mathop{\mathrm{SL}}_n$, over every number field.*

</div>

The formulation, including the results for multiplicative groups of division algebras that enter the inner type $A$ case, is recalled in <a id="pm-cite-24"></a>[[PRsurvey](#ref-PRsurvey), Sections 3.4–3.5].
